# Migration plan: cockpit to oculus

Execution ledger for `tools/oculus/DESIGN.md`. One bullet is one package of work and one agent dispatch, except the bookkeeping packages the orchestrator does itself (marked). A package is ticked only after its verification commands ran and the orchestrator read the whole diff; the commands are in `orchestrator-state.md`, and nothing is ticked on inspection. The green checkmark is this file's status vocabulary by Axel's instruction of 2026-09-10; everything else in this folder is ASCII. Read `orchestrator-state.md` first after any context compaction. The lettering C, R, S, O is the ledger's own; the cockpit roadmap's letters A to I map onto the O packages as noted.

## Packages, in execution order

One line per package: the status mark, the id, the title, and where the rest is (the brief, the review, the landing note in `orchestrator-state.md`). Lean by Axel's rule of 2026-09-12; nothing about a package lives only here.

After the rulings M8 still waits for D23c to land, since both add a folder to `src/lib/server/machine/lsp/`, which holds 10 items, and both change `relay.ts` (869 of 880 lines). (1) The key that names a reference to another file: the hub's 16 `schema:` blocks write `options_from:`, `manifest-schema.yml` writes `from:`, `options.yml` writes `values_from:`. Options: (a) recommended: `from:` everywhere, renaming `options_from` in the 16 hub files, in the readers, in the apps API (`optionsFrom`, `docs/apps.md:247`) and in the cockpit selftest (`tools/cockpit/src/cockpit/selftest.py:595-621`); pro: one name for one thing, as M8's line names it; con: the apps API changes and the cockpit check is edited. (b) keep each file's name; pro: nothing renamed; con: three names for one thing (the lazy option). (2) What a `from:` names: today a bare word that is sometimes a registry (`people`) and sometimes a vocab list (`venue_categories`). Options: (a) recommended: a registry by its `sources.yml` name (`from: people`) and a vocab list with a prefix (`from: vocab.venue_categories`); pro: each file path stays written once, in `sources.yml`, and the two kinds cannot clash; con: every vocab reference is rewritten. (b) a file path and key field (`from: { file: registries/people.yml, key: key }`); pro: explicit; con: the path is written in every schema. (c) today's bare words (the lazy option). (3) How a condition is written (`when:`), which no file has today: conditions live only in help text and header comments. Options: (a) recommended: a map of field to value or list of values, all of which must hold for the field to be allowed (`when: { category: conference }`); a field that is required only under a condition writes `required: { when: ... }`; a condition on a project's tier (which is a folder, not a field) is written as a path pattern (`when: { path: projects/archive/** }`); pro: readable YAML, maps one to one onto JSON Schema's `if`/`then`; con: no "or" between fields. (b) a string expression (`when: "category == 'conference'"`); pro: any condition; con: a small language to parse and document. (c) raw JSON Schema `if`/`then` in the YAML; con: unreadable in a registry file. Lazy option: conditions left in help text. (4) `options.yml` calls the help line `description:` (202 times) and the allowed values `values:`, where the other schemas say `help:` and `options:`. Options: (a) recommended: rename them to `help:` and `options:`; pro: one name per thing across all schemas; con: a rename across `options.yml` and its readers (`catalog/options.ts`, `value-rule.ts`). (b) keep them and translate in the generator (the lazy option). (5) Where the three new schemas go. Options: (a) recommended: `registries/career.yml` and `feeds/literature-radar.yml` get their own `schema:` block at the top as the other 16 hub files have, and the side projects' schema is a new file `config/catalogs/side-project-schema.yml` beside `manifest-schema.yml` (the folder goes from 8 to 9 items); career is a map of sections, so its block gains a nested field form (`fields:` under a field); pro: each file carries its own schema as the rest of the hub does; con: a nested form added to the schema format. (b) all three as files in `config/catalogs/`, which makes 11 items and forces a subfolder that also moves `manifest-schema.yml`. (c) one `config/catalogs/schemas.yml` for the three. (6) Whether oculus's own checks (the Findings view and the real-hub test G14) apply the same schemas as the editor. Options: (a) recommended: oculus validates the hub through the generated JSON Schemas with ajv 8.20.0 (MIT, maintained by blakeembrey and esp, last release 2026-04-24) as a new direct dependency, so a value the editor marks is also a finding, and the hand-written `REFERENCES` table in `validate.ts:83-132` is replaced; pro: one rule for both sides, as Z468's "checked" asks; con: a new dependency and a rewrite of part of `validate.ts`. (b) a hand-written check in `validate.ts` reading `from:` and `when:`; con: two implementations of one rule. (c) editor marks only (the lazy option); con: the Findings view and G14 never see a wrong value. (7) The data departures Z468 says M8 settles, each value Axel's data: (7a) `registries/data.yml:77`, the `pmc-patients` set has `access: public`, not a `data_access` value; recommended: change it to `open`, since the set needs no credentials; alternative: add `public` to `data_access`. (7b) `outcome` and `doi` are written on 18 manifests outside the archive tier (12 under `projects/coauthor/published/`, and `active/linfoot-estimator`, `coauthor/abandoned/individual-reference-intervals`, `coauthor/active/{clinical-hccnet,elixir-fl4e,flkit}`, `manuscripts/erp-signatures`), where `outcome` holds the involvement statement; the card already shows `outcome` on every tier (`CardDetails.svelte:72-74`); recommended: allow both fields on every tier and rename the section "Archive (archive tier)" to say so; alternatives: move the text to another field, or delete it (data loss, the lazy option). (7c) the journal `hcin` (`registries/venues.yml:245`) carries `timing`, `deadline` and `call` for a topical collection; recommended: allow those three on every venue and keep the month fields conference-only; alternative: drop them from `hcin` (loses the 2026-12-02 deadline). (7d) the Graz BCI event (`network/events.yml:62-73`) has `role: present` and no `link`, since a co-author presents; recommended: under (3)(a) `link` is allowed for `role: present`, not required, so the entry is valid as it is; alternative: a new role value for a talk a co-author gives. (7e) the venues `kuleuven-thesis`, `uhasselt-thesis` and `xai-nl` have no `category`; recommended: add `thesis` and `meeting` to `venue_categories` and give them those values, which adds two groups to the Venues view; alternative: file them under `journal` or `conference`, which is false. (7f) `registries/data.yml` uses eight retrieval methods (`direct` 1, `library` 9, `manual` 28, `physionet` 3, `physionet_zip` 2, `private` 4, `script` 4, `zenodo` 3) while its comment at `:9` names four; recommended: the eight in use become the allowed values and the comment is rewritten; `tools/data/redownload.py:217-240` prints "unknown retrieval.method ... skipping" for `direct` and `script`, which is a defect of the hub's data tool outside oculus, put to Axel as a roadmap entry (`/roadmap add`) and not built in M8. (8) Which YAML files M8 covers. Options: (a) recommended: the files M8's line names, plus the 26 deck `present.yml` files (with a schema in `config/catalogs/` written after PR3 lands, since PR3 changes their `slides:` and `order:`) and `.oculus/cpp.yml` (D23's compile database file), since the YAML editor opens all of them; pro: every YAML file oculus reads gets completion and marks; con: two more schemas and a wait on PR3. (b) only the files the line names (the lazy option). D41 asks for three things that Microsoft's Markdown language server already does, the same server that gives VS Code its Markdown features: a mark on a link to a file that does not exist, Cmd+click on a link to open its file, and, when a file or folder is moved, the edits that rewrite every Markdown link pointing into it (its request `markdown/getEditForFileRenames`). The package is `vscode-markdown-languageserver` 0.5.0 (MIT, Microsoft, released 2026-07-16, Node 22 or later; oculus runs Node 24), built on `vscode-markdown-languageservice` 0.5.0. To run it, oculus answers its custom requests the way the relay already answers the YAML server's schema requests: `markdown/parse` (the tokens of a file, which the server expects from markdown-it, `markdown-it` 15.0.2, MIT, a new dependency, since oculus renders with `marked`), the file reads `markdown/fs/readFile`, `readDirectory` and `stat`, the watchers, and `markdown/findMarkdownFilesInWorkspace`, each kept inside the served roots. The backticked hub paths (`` `docs/DATA-MAP.md` `` in prose) are not Markdown links, so no library marks or opens them; that part, the Audit item for a wrong `type` and the two defects from M7 are hand-written whatever the choice. The library is a choice the rules leave to you, so the brief is not written until you rule.

Changing G3's sentence touches the secrets rule, which automode leaves to Axel. (a) Recommended: G3 reads "... is given to the YAML or Markdown language server, ...", with the Markdown entry's refusal tests as its rows; pro: the refusal is held by a test like the YAML server's; con: none found. (b) G3 unchanged and the refusal built with no test, the lazy option; con: a later change could hand the server a secrets file with no test going red. Every other finding of the check is closed in the brief or as Z743; the brief is written again at the next /spec with those changes.
    - (a) Recommended: the Markdown language server as one more served language in the relay (`.md` tabs), with the custom requests answered by the relay, markdown-it added for `markdown/parse`, the Files view's move and rename asking the server for `markdown/getEditForFileRenames` and showing the edit in the Refactor Preview D39 built, and the backticked paths answered by an entry hook as D39's hub keys are. Pros: VS Code's own behaviour and wording for link checks, Cmd+click and link updates, and with it heading links, link completion, document symbols, Find All References to a heading or a file, and rename of a heading; maintained by the VS Code team. Cons: one more child process per served root while a Markdown tab is open; two new dependencies; the README calls the server "still in development" for clients other than VS Code, so the relay's answers to its custom requests need their own tests.
    - (b) `vscode-markdown-languageservice` used as a library inside the oculus server, with no separate process. Pros: no child process; the same link features. Cons: oculus then hosts by hand what the server package already wires (the document store, the diagnostics schedule, the workspace), against how every other language is served in oculus (`server/machine/lsp/`), and still needs markdown-it.
    - (c) Write it by hand: a link scanner over `marked`'s tokens, a definition answer and a rewrite of links on a move. Pros: no new dependency. Cons: every rule of Markdown link resolution (reference links, angle-bracket and percent-encoded paths, `#` fragments, links in HTML) is written and tested again, and it covers less than (a); this is the lazy option.

Cause: two `vite build`s in one checkout at once. `tests/tiers/landing.mjs` runs `npm run test:slow` in a lane beside `npm run check; npm run build`, and since O30 every app start from the checkout builds the server into `.svelte-kit/output` and `build/`; the lane's build empties `.svelte-kit/output` while the app's build reads it, the app shows `desktop.dialog.build_failed`, the harness never answers a dialog, and `printed()` waits to the 600000 ms limit. Fix for the new brief: (A) `landing.mjs` runs `test:slow` in the lane after the type check and the build; (B) `printed()` in `start.test.ts` fails as soon as the app records a dialog before the awaited text and prints the app's output; (C) the dialog's reason is the last line that names an error, not the last line of the output (`src/desktop/server/build.ts:32`, `process.ts:65`); and the landing fixes of `agents/reviews/O30-gate.patch`.   - Step 0, stopped at /spec 2026-10-06: PR3's PDF export needs a library choice (the library rule: the orchestrator checks, Axel decides). Oculus has no PDF writer today (`grep -rn "printToPDF\|jspdf\|pdf-lib\|window.print" src` prints nothing). Options: (a) no new dependency: a page route draws every slide of the deck in its 1280 by 720 frame, one per printed page (`@page` sized 16:9), and Export to PDF prints it; the desktop app writes the PDF straight into the deck's folder through Electron's own `webContents.printToPDF` (Electron 44.5.1 is already a dependency), a browser tab opens the browser's print dialog with Save as PDF. For: text and KaTeX stay vector and selectable, the slides are drawn by the same components as on screen, nothing to install. Against: in a browser tab the reader passes the print dialog and picks where the file goes. (b) playwright-core 1.63.0 (Apache-2.0, Microsoft, released 2026-10-06) or puppeteer-core 25.12.0 (Apache-2.0, Google, 2026-09-23): the server drives a Chromium to print that route to `<deck>/<name>.pdf`. For: one press writes the file in a browser tab too. Against: needs a Chrome binary found on the machine (or Electron's), a process the server starts under the processes rule, and a large dependency for one control. (c) modern-screenshot 4.7.0 (MIT, 2026-04-16) with jsPDF 4.2.1 (MIT, 2026-03-17) in the page: each slide rasterised and placed on a page. For: one press, no process. Against: text is pictures (not selectable, not searchable), KaTeX and fonts must be embedded right, and the file is large. Recommendation (a): vector output from the slides as drawn, no dependency; (c) is the lazy option for quality. Ruled 2026-10-06 by Axel: (a), Z665.

- Z680 (2026-10-07, PR4's /spec, the orchestrator, automode): the presenter screen's shape. (1) A Presenter control in the Present view's toolbar beside Present, and the key P while the deck has the focus, start it: the pane becomes the presenter screen and the audience window opens. (2) The audience window is the server's route `/present/show?root=&path=`, opened by the page's `window.open` in both hosts: in a browser a second browser window, which the reader moves to the projector and puts in full screen with F or a click on its Full Screen control (a browser enters full screen only from a press in that window); in the desktop app the host opens it as an app window of its own, full screen on a display other than the main window's when there is one, and as a window beside the main window when there is none. (3) The two windows keep one slide through a BroadcastChannel named by the deck; arrows, Space, PageUp, PageDown and a click move both from either window, so a clicker works whichever window has the focus. (4) The presenter screen draws the current slide large, the next slide smaller beside it (the closing words "End of the deck" after the last), the current slide's notes under the next slide as plain text with their line breaks ("No notes for this slide." when it has none), the slide counter, the time of day, and a timer counting from the Presenter press with Pause, Resume and Reset. (5) While the presenter screen is on, both windows draw the deck as saved, never a source tab's unsaved text, so the audience sees what the presenter sees; a save redraws both. (6) End in the presenter screen, Esc there, or closing the audience window ends it: the audience window closes and the pane is the Present view again at the slide shown. (7) The audience window may call no bridge channel, is no entry of the windows list and is never written to the window file, so it is not restored at the next start. Not chosen, the lazy options: a presenter screen in the browser only; notes in a separate tab with no sync; the slide kept in step by the saved pane state, which ties the audience to one pane's id. Not chosen, others: a new bridge channel to open the audience window, which a browser host lacks, where `window.open` serves both hosts; drafts drawn on the presenter only, which shows the presenter a slide the audience does not see.

- Z685 (2026-10-07, PR4's /spec, the orchestrator, automode): the choices PR4's brief check raises (`agents/reviews/PR4-brief-check.md`), each its recommendation. (1) Presenting is held in a page store keyed by the pane's instance id, never in the saved layout, so it survives a tab switch, a page switch and any redraw of the panes, and ends on End, Esc, the audience window closed, the tab or pane closed, or a reload; no layout key is added. (2) The audience learns the presenter is gone through a Web Lock the presenter page holds for the deck while presenting (`navigator.locks`), released when the page goes, plus an "end" message on End and on `pagehide`; no beat and no silence timer, so a hidden or throttled presenter never ends the talk. (3) One presenter per deck: Presenter on a second pane over the same deck takes the audience window over and the first pane's presenter screen ends. (4) The deck switched in the toolbar while presenting ends the presentation of the old deck. (5) In the desktop app the audience window covers its display with simple full screen (`setSimpleFullScreen` on macOS), so no Space is made and the presenter's display stays usable. (6) In the desktop app the host makes the audience window itself (the page's `window.open` answers null there): an existing audience window of that deck is brought forward instead of a second, it is placed against the window that asked (a float window included) and closes with it, its navigations and its own `window.open` go through `linkDecision` as G47 says, it gets the full screen permission, and Close Window with it focused closes it. (7) The key P is named in the Presenter control's tip and in `present.key_hint`; `present.present_tip` keeps naming F; the audience window draws no key hint. (8) A click on the presenter screen's current slide moves to the next slide; a click on the next slide's preview or the notes does nothing. (9) The audience page whose deck read fails reloads itself once, then shows the end sentence. (10) Close on the audience page is shown only when the page can close its window, otherwise the sentence says to close the tab. Not chosen, the lazy options: the state in the component (a page switch ends the talk); the 5 s silence rule; a second audience window per pane; native full screen that blanks the other displays; the desktop half left to the look.
- Z686 (2026-10-07, D23b's /spec, the orchestrator, automode): the choices D23b's brief check raises (`agents/reviews/D23b-brief-check.md`), each its recommendation. (1) The Svelte server starts for every repository with `isTrusted: false` (no repository config, no repository package loaded), and is restarted trusted only after the processes approval of the text of that repository's `svelte.config.*` and `vite.config.*` files, kept in the approvals store keyed by that text as `DESIGN.md:165` keys an approval, so a changed file asks again; this replaces Z682 (6)'s "does not start without the approval". (5) A server's page-facing facts (languages, label keys, semantic tokens, format-on-type, its own commands and choice kinds) live in one shared module under `src/lib/data/`, which `servers.ts` and the page both read by the server's id; `servers.ts:6` names the two places; the label keys' prefix joins `COMPUTED_PREFIXES` in `tests/checks/source/keys.test.ts`, reported. (10) The History label has a singular form, "Edited by a language server in 1 file". Monaco's code action provider (`node_modules/monaco-editor/esm/external/monaco-lsp-client/out/index.js:2055-2067`) sends no `context.only` and declares no `providedCodeActionKinds`, so `asksOrganize` (`kit/lsp/transport/messages.ts:131`) never sees a request and Organize Imports is unreachable for pyright and clangd on the tree. Not chosen: two Reverts with different results, the lazy option.
